# 26: The Autumn Outing

"Ka-kah!!"

"I am fear! I am the end of the world!"

Gulp gulp gulp—

With a giant gulp, Gastly downed a large mouthful of "Moomoo Milk."

Johan watched this scene with exasperation. If only he could hit Gastly, he'd definitely give it a flick on the head. Unlike other Gastly, which were lethargic during the day and lively at night, his Gastly snored louder than him at night and became incredibly hyperactive by morning.

Completely oblivious to Johan's glare from behind, Gastly continued clutching the half-full bottle of "Moomoo Milk" in its gaseous hands, eyes gleaming as it glanced at the three boxes stacked in the corner. Gastly felt like this was peak happiness—true bliss in its ghostly life.

"Alright, alright, there's going to be plenty of tasty treats from now on," Johan said, bread hanging from his mouth. He casually grabbed a bottle of Moomoo Milk from the box, opened it, and took a sip.

His eyes brightened.

He had to admit, the flavor was really good—sweet but not too rich, with an added refreshing effect. No wonder so many trainers and Pokémon were crazy about this milk.

"Ka...kah."

Gastly stared blankly at Johan.

M-m-my milk…

Noticing Gastly's mournful gaze, Johan paused and then sheepishly replied, "Uh, I was in a rush this morning and didn't have time to make breakfast, so I drank one bottle. I'll make it up to you with a whole box next time, okay?"

"Ka-kah..."

Hopefully...

Johan hoisted a small black backpack onto his back, opened the door, and headed out.

Today was the academy's planned autumn outing. Since they would be gone for several days, Johan had spent the entire morning preparing for the trip, leaving no time for breakfast.

Though his backpack looked small, it was packed with plenty of items, including a tent in case they needed to camp. In the world of Pokémon, space-compression technology was highly advanced.

From Poké Balls to backpacks, the technology had become quite sophisticated. Even Johan's seemingly unremarkable, lightweight bag could hold a lot of things.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gastly quickly finished off the remaining milk and floated after Johan.

Rustboro Pokémon Academy

Several well-kept buses were already parked at the entrance to the academy. While students hadn't arrived yet, many of the teachers were waiting.

"Mr. Johan."

A muscular middle-aged man with a buzz cut and a tight vest approached Johan with a smile.

"Mr. Dolf."

Johan responded with his standard smile, nodding in acknowledgment.

This brawny man was Dolf, the homeroom teacher for first-year Class 1 in the battling department. Dolf was once quite well-known, having participated in numerous tournaments, including the Ever Grande Conference, though his rankings weren't particularly high.

"Ms. Saiko, Ms. Shelly," Johan greeted the two female teachers who were joining them.

Both responded politely. Saiko seemed familiar with Johan and greeted him warmly, while Shelly appeared more reserved.

But Johan didn't mind—he preferred Shelly's cold indifference. The less attention he got from her, the better.

He had no intention of getting on Team Aqua's radar.

"Here are the travel itineraries and some important reminders," Dolf said, handing over several sheets of paper to the three of them. He went on to briefly explain the details before smiling, "I hope we can have a smooth collaboration."

The four of them were the teachers in charge of Class 1's autumn outing from the battling department.

Soon, the students began arriving.

Johan's task was simple: checking off the names of students as they boarded the bus.

"Mr. Johan!"

"Mr. Johan, I didn't expect to see you here!"

Steven and Roxanne, who arrived together, were visibly surprised upon spotting Johan.

Johan stared at Steven for a few seconds. Seeing that his surprise seemed genuine, Johan abandoned the idea that Steven might have influenced the arrangement.

It appeared that Johan's participation in the autumn outing was purely the academy's decision—a stroke of bad luck that placed him in the mix.

"Get on the bus," Johan waved them aboard.

The two quickly complied.

But just as Steven was about to board, Johan suddenly called out, "Steven, thanks."

Steven paused for a moment, then quickly caught on, flashing a bright smile. "I should be the one thanking you, Mr. Johan."

Roxanne looked confused, but Johan and Steven both understood.

The rapid rise in popularity of Johan's academic paper, even earning a comment from Professor Oak, was no accident. Someone had definitely "boosted" it, and the one responsible was undoubtedly Steven.

Of course, Steven hadn't blindly boosted the paper—he'd read it first and believed it was insightful and innovative. Otherwise, even with a spike in attention, the hype would have fizzled quickly.

Professor Oak's comment only further validated the merit of Johan's research. That was the true reason it gained so much attention.

"Mr. Johan, are all the students accounted for?" Dolf asked from the bus, noticing other buses had already begun to leave.

"One more," Johan replied, scanning the list.

No sooner had the words left his mouth when—

"Wait for me!!"

Clip-clop, clip-clop!

The rapid sound of hooves approached.

A red-haired girl rode up on a flaming pony. It was Flannery and her Ponyta—the last to arrive.

Johan checked off the final name on his list.

The bus slowly pulled away.

Their destination: Lavaridge Town, nestled beneath Mt. Chimney, in the heart of the Hoenn region.

With Rustboro City and Lavaridge Town being quite a distance apart, it would take several hours to get there.

Class 1 wasn't very large, so there were plenty of empty seats on the bus. Johan chose to sit in the back.

This allowed Gastly some room to move around without disturbing others.

Hmm… where's Gastly?

Johan had just settled into his seat when he suddenly realized Gastly was missing.

Meanwhile…

On top of the bus…

"Ka—kah—(too windy!)"

Gastly: (;Д｀)

I'm shrinking…

Johan: "..."

By the time Gastly floated back inside, it had shrunk to two-thirds of its usual size. It grinned sheepishly at Johan.

"Ka...kaah."

As a gaseous Pokémon, 95% of Gastly's body was made up of toxic gas, allowing it to slip into almost any space. But in the face of strong winds, if it couldn't gather enough gas, its body would shrink.

Fortunately, Gastly would be back to its full size after resting for a while.

At least no one had noticed Gastly's blunder. Silently, Johan stuffed the ghost back into his pocket, pulled out his phone, and casually browsed the news.

"Jubilife TV's 'Jubilife Tournament' wraps up as Trainer Cynthia takes the crown. Garchomp dominates with a clean sweep. Video link: ..."

"A massive Graveler discovered outside Mauville City, suspected to be…"

Mauville City?

After dealing with the remnants of Greater Mauville Holdings and the plot against Steven, Johan had become a little sensitive to anything related to Mauville.

"Mauville City isn't too close to Mt. Chimney or Lavaridge Town, though…"

As Johan pondered, he noticed the website's chat feature blinking.

Curious, he opened it.

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: Feebas seems more energetic after eating the Apicot Berry. (Feebas video attached

"It's good it doesn't mind the sour taste. That makes things a bit easier."

Johan stroked his chin thoughtfully and then replied:

[Grain in the Field]: In that case, try making Pokéblocks or Poffins with Apicot Berries. The effects should be even better. But keep in mind that with only physical treatment, this could take a while—and might cost a lot. Be prepared for that.

Considering the person had casually transferred 20,000 Poké Dollars, they probably wouldn't mind spending a bit more on Pokéblocks.

After a moment, Steven suddenly came over from the front of the bus and sat down next to Johan.

"Mr. Johan, are you planning to go to the hot springs tonight?"

---

Hoenn Region, Lavaridge Town.

One by one, the buses parked in front of the Lavaridge Town Pokémon Center. Under the supervision of the teachers, the students slowly disembarked and began moving their luggage inside.

As per the school's arrangements, the students would stay at the Pokémon Center for the first night. Each room would house two students, and this applied to the teachers as well.

However, since there was an odd number of students in the first-year battling class, Steven made a request for Johan to share a room with him, instead of the original arrangement where Johan would have bunked with their homeroom teacher, Dolf.

Truth be told, Johan wasn't entirely thrilled about sharing a room with another man, but since it was the school's arrangement, and even the Devon Corporation heir Steven had no objections, Johan decided he should set a good example as a teacher.

Besides, compared to the muscled-up Dolf... it was much easier to accept sharing with Steven.

In addition, Johan was much closer in age to Steven than Dolf.

Once everyone settled into their rooms, Dolf announced that the afternoon and evening would be free time, much to the students' delight.

Of course, free time meant they could only explore Lavaridge Town itself. If anyone wanted to leave town, they needed the teachers' approval.

As noon approached, the students grouped up and left the Pokémon Center to find food. Johan, too, was invited by Steven, Roxanne, and Flannery.

"Mr. Johan, would you like to join us for lunch?" Roxanne asked, still holding a book in her arms, even during the autumn outing.

Before Johan could decline, she added, "Flannery knows a great restaurant—it's one of Lavaridge Town's specialties!"

Flannery was a native of Lavaridge Town, and her grandfather, a former member of the Hoenn Elite Four, was currently the town's Gym Leader.

"Ka-kah?"

Gastly, still shrunken down and not fully recovered from earlier, popped its head out, its eyes gleaming with interest.

In the end, faced with the expectant gazes of Steven, Roxanne, and Flannery, Johan agreed to join them.

It wasn't that he was particularly tight with his money... Okay, maybe he was a little stingy. After all, he'd already dropped enough hints to Steven once, and that was enough.

Even though these three students were likely wealthier than him, as a teacher, it didn't feel right to let the students pay for his meal.

Seeing Johan agree, Flannery exchanged a sly glance with Steven and Roxanne, satisfied with their little success.

Once they stepped out of the Pokémon Center, the heat hit them like a wave.

A few more steps, and they could see a towering mountain in the distance—none other than Mt. Chimney, one of Hoenn's iconic landmarks.

Mt. Chimney, a 1,500-meter-tall volcano located in the heart of Hoenn, hadn't erupted in many years, but it continued to emit high temperatures, remaining a dormant volcano.

From afar, dark volcanic ash could be seen drifting down like black snow.

As it fell to a certain altitude, the ash seemed to resemble large black feathers swirling in the wind. Carried by mountain breezes, it drifted toward the northeast.

It was easy to imagine how the thick volcanic ash could impact the surrounding environment.

However, despite being located at the base of Mt. Chimney, Lavaridge Town was on the southern side of the mountain. Thanks to the mountain winds, the ash rarely reached the town, causing minimal disturbance.

Instead, the high temperatures from the dormant volcano had given Lavaridge Town its famous "volcanic hot springs," attracting many visitors.

'Is that where the "non-flying one" sleeps?' Johan thought as he gazed at the towering, cloud-wreathed mountain.

If that beast ever awoke, it would be...

But he quickly dismissed the thought with a wry smile.

How unlucky would someone have to be to run into Groudon on a casual trip to Mt. Chimney?

Besides, Groudon was still slumbering. Without the "Red Orb," it wouldn't wake up, even if someone danced on its head.

"Mr. Johan?"

Steven, Roxanne, and Flannery had walked a bit ahead when they noticed Johan standing still, staring at Mt. Chimney, lost in thought.

Snapping back to reality, Johan adjusted his jacket and followed them at a leisurely pace.

He had to admit, Gastly was incredibly useful in this sweltering environment—thanks to Gastly, Johan's body temperature was at least five degrees cooler, making the heat far more bearable.

"Ka-kah—"

See how great I am now?

In a small, relatively secluded restaurant in Lavaridge Town, Gastly lay comfortably on a soft cushion, letting out a satisfied burp after the meal.

Johan, who had just returned from paying the bill, couldn't help but twitch at the sight.

Gastly had eaten the most out of anyone!

Second place went to Flannery, who had insisted they come to this restaurant, followed by Steven and Johan, with Roxanne eating the least.

But even Johan had to admit, the food here was quite good.

Especially the hot spring egg rice bowl, made with genuine volcanic hot spring eggs.

"Now that we're full, what should we do this afternoon?" Flannery leaned back in her chair, her gaze bouncing between Johan, Steven, and Roxanne, looking slightly bored.

It was pretty clear what Flannery wanted to suggest, especially with the not-so-subtle glances she kept throwing at Steven and Roxanne.

Steven sighed, while Roxanne turned her head away, pretending not to notice.

"How about we head up Mt. Chimney!" Flannery suddenly slapped the table, full of energy as she continued her act.

"Ka-kah!"

Before Johan could react, Gastly, who was feeling sleepy from the big meal, was startled out of its daze.

It glared at Flannery with a "suspicious" look.

This girl had been the one who fought the hardest over the food earlier!

"What do you think? I know a great spot on Mt. Chimney, and there are plenty of wild Pokémon there too!" Flannery pressed on, eager to show off her "local knowledge."

Seeing Flannery so enthusiastic, Steven and Roxanne felt bad about shooting her down, so they turned their hopeful gazes toward Johan.

Johan, however, remained unfazed. He calmly sipped his tea before replying, "The free activity zone is limited to Lavaridge Town. Mt. Chimney isn't included."

"As long as we get your approval, we're good to go. That's why I'm asking you, Mr. Johan," Flannery pleaded, still hopeful.

"No."

Johan's refusal was swift and firm.

"I promise it's not dangerous!"

"No."

"It's not far from Lavaridge Town. I used to go there all the time when I was younger."

"No."

"…"

No matter how much Flannery begged, Johan's answer remained a calm but unwavering "no."

Her stubbornness was clearly starting to wear thin.

"I'll introduce you to my best friend! She's super pretty!" Flannery finally resorted to her trump card.

"No—wait, what?"

Johan froze, and even Steven and Roxanne were taken aback.

"Ahem, still no," Johan coughed lightly, regaining his composure.

"You don't even know who my friend is! She's—" Flannery stomped her foot in frustration. "Fine! I challenge you to a battle! If I win, you let us go up the mountain!"

Then she quickly added, "You're not scared, are you?"

Johan chuckled. "That won't work on me."

Flannery scratched her head in frustration.

When she finally ran out of ideas, she pouted and muttered, "Fine, if you won't agree, we'll just sneak out later. No one would even notice."

That, however, did have an effect.

Johan turned to Steven. "Steven, do you want to go up the mountain too?"

Caught in Johan's gaze, Steven hesitated before sheepishly admitting, "I heard there's a rare type of volcanic stone up there…"

Johan sighed and turned back to Flannery, his tone calm. "Alright, I'll accept your challenge. If you win, you can go up the mountain. But if you lose, you'll have to listen to me."

"However, as you know, I'm just an ordinary Pokémon psychology teacher, and my Gastly was only recently caught. You wouldn't want to be accused of bullying the weak, would you?"

Hearing this, Roxanne and Steven exchanged a glance, both inwardly thinking, This won't end well.

Before they could warn her, Flannery confidently puffed out her chest and declared, "No problem! I also have a Pokémon I just caught recently!"

Smack!

Both Steven and Roxanne facepalmed at the same time.

Flannery hadn't fallen for Johan's reverse psychology, but she had walked right into his trap.

"Ka-kah?"

Of course, the most confused one was Gastly.

How did this conversation suddenly turn to involve me?

---

Lavaridge Town.

Not far from the restaurant stood a rather remote, somewhat dilapidated battle arena.

As Flannery gazed at the old arena, her eyes reflected a bit of nostalgia.

She had many fond memories here.

However, she quickly put aside her sentimentality, her enthusiasm surging as she returned to being the "girl burning with passion."

Others might not have noticed this shift, but Johan certainly did.

Come to think of it, whether in the original story or in what he knew of Flannery, apart from mentioning her grandfather, she rarely, if ever, spoke about her parents.

"Mr. Johan, I won't hold back!" Flannery declared, full of energy.

On the sidelines, Steven and Roxanne exchanged concerned looks over Flannery's "situation."

For a trainer like Flannery, who let emotions rule over reason, it seemed like she was about to face her worst possible opponent—someone like Johan, a Pokémon psychologist.

If Flannery had chosen to use her strongest Pokémon, perhaps she could rely on sheer strength to win. But...

"Ka-kah, hic—Ka-kah?"

Floating before Johan, Gastly let out a burp and turned to him with a questioning look.

Can't I just take her out right away?

"You can try," Johan replied, his eye twitching slightly before he added, "but be ready to face a former Elite Four member afterward."

"Ka-kah!"

Gastly: (°ロ°)

At that moment, Flannery threw out a Poké Ball, and in a flash of red light, a Pokémon appeared on the field.

It resembled a black dog, with prominent bone-like structures on its back, head, and ankles, giving the once-cute creature a more menacing look. Its belly and muzzle were orange, and a short, stubby tail extended behind it.

It was a Dark- and Fire-type Pokémon—Houndour!

Seeing this Pokémon, both Steven and Roxanne were a bit surprised.

They had expected Flannery to send out her Numel, a recently captured Pokémon as well.

However, Numel, a Fire- and Ground-type, would have been at a disadvantage against Gastly, but Houndour, with its Dark and Fire attributes... now that was a clear advantage.

After all, Gastly, being both Ghost- and Poison-type, was weak to Dark-type moves—Houndour's specialty.

Moreover, if Flannery's Houndour had the "Early Bird" ability, it would give her an even greater edge!

With such a lineup, there might actually be a chance for her to win!

After all, Mr. Johan wasn't a professional trainer, and he likely didn't have much battle experience. If he only had theoretical knowledge...

Flannery grinned excitedly. Being emotional didn't necessarily mean being foolish.

Steven took a deep breath and called out, "Let the battle begin!"

Flannery dramatically pointed her arm, directing her Houndour.

"Houndour, Bite!"

"Arf!!"

Houndour barked fiercely, its four legs moving swiftly as dark, sinister energy began to swirl around its still-developing fangs.

At the same time, in Johan's vision, Houndour's stats appeared before him.

[Houndour]

[Type: Dark, Fire]

[Ability: Flash Fire]

[Moves: Leer, Ember, Howl, Smog, Roar, Bite, Fire Spin, Fire Fang]

[Mood: Excited, Worried (I have a bad feeling about this...)]

[Condition: Excellent (Well-trained, in peak condition)]

[Friendship: 5 (Has some goodwill toward humans because of Flannery)]

"Gastly, use Scary Face," Johan calmly instructed, his voice even.

Though Gastly had been lazily floating around just moments ago, the Pokémon instantly snapped into focus, as if the previous lethargy had been a ruse.

As a gas-type Pokémon, Gastly was incredibly fast. In the blink of an eye, its body swelled like a balloon, transforming into a giant, grotesque face.

"Ka-kah!"

The eerie laugh echoed as dark mist twisted and shifted, unsettling the young Houndour.

Johan sighed internally.

Why did trainers of Dark-type Pokémon always insist on opening with "Bite"?

Did they really believe type advantage determined everything?

This wasn't a game, and move accuracy wasn't just based on hit rates or evasion stats.

Did they think the opponent wouldn't exploit their weaknesses?

Though flustered, Houndour still managed to snap its jaws shut.

Bang!

Teeth clashed with teeth, and black mist seeped through the gaps.

All it had bitten was a small wisp of the gaseous body Gastly had inflated into a giant face.

On the sidelines, Steven and Roxanne exchanged a glance.

Earlier, when Flannery had made the first move, Johan had responded almost simultaneously with his command. This allowed Gastly, with its eerie Scary Face move, to capitalize on the timing and shake Houndour's confidence.

Simultaneous commands...

Could it be that Johan had predicted Flannery's strategy from the start?

Was he reading her moves ahead of time?

Could this really be the teacher with little battle experience?

Or was this the tactical prowess of a Pokémon psychologist?

They had expected Johan to rely on Gastly's tricky (and frankly, annoying) moves to gain the upper hand. But to think it would play out like this...

"Use Curse."

Pokémon battles weren't turn-based, so with Houndour having missed its attack, Johan wouldn't let this opening slip by.

"Ka-kah—"

Gastly let out a sinister laugh as an invisible, eerie energy seemed to sink into Houndour's body, planting a seed of dread that quickly began to grow.

"Houndour, we need to finish this fast!" Flannery ordered, her expression turning serious. She was clearly aware of Curse's effects.

She waved her arm again.

"Use Leer! Then charge in!"

"Gastly, Hypnosis."

Once again, their voices rang out nearly simultaneously.

Houndour widened its eyes menacingly, trying to intimidate its opponent. However...

What happens when you open your eyes wide in front of Hypnosis?

Houndour's body trembled, and after taking a few shaky steps forward, it collapsed onto the ground, fast asleep.

"Get up! Houndour!" Flannery's face flushed red as she shouted.

Johan stared at the fallen Houndour for a few moments.

When the energy of the Curse began to sap away some of Houndour's strength, with no sign of it waking up, Johan finally relaxed a little.

Thank goodness.

Clearly, the bond between Flannery and her Houndour wasn't strong enough to break Hypnosis with just a command.

To be honest, Johan hadn't realized until this battle just how much he feared trainers like Flannery—the kind that didn't play by the rules, yet somehow managed to break the norm.

The only way to handle such trainers was with someone even more unpredictable.

Like... a Water-absorbing Charizard.

"It's over," Johan declared, calling an end to the battle.

"We haven't even—" Flannery tried to protest.

"Gastly."

"Ka-kah~~"

In response, Gastly unleashed a rapid series of moves—Confuse Ray, Lick, Scary Face, Will-O-Wisp, Smog...

Flannery's words died in her throat.

A bead of sweat rolled down her forehead.

"You should call Houndour back before Curse affects it any more. Otherwise, you'll be heading straight to the Pokémon Center," Johan advised calmly, as Gastly floated lazily back to his side.

Meeting Johan's gaze, Gastly blinked and whispered in a low voice.

"Ka-kah~~"

Don't worry, I used the regular Hypnosis.

Gastly: I'm not that dumb. You said her grandpa is a former Elite Four member.

Johan nodded in relief.

He didn't want Houndour to develop any psychological issues. Otherwise, he'd have to be the one to treat it.

"We lost..." Flannery reluctantly accepted the outcome.

But Johan's interest had been piqued—not so much in Flannery herself, but in trainers like her, the ones who were more emotional than logical.

It was clear that Flannery and Houndour still had a ways to go in terms of teamwork. There was a noticeable gap between her commands and her Pokémon's actions. Johan was curious to see if, after more bonding, they might break through some of the usual limits.

After all, that was a key area of research for his predecessor.

As the battle ended, Steven and Roxanne walked over.

Roxanne tried to console Flannery, but to her surprise, Flannery wasn't nearly as devastated as expected. She quickly regained her enthusiasm.

Looking at Johan, she beamed, "Mr. Johan, I'll challenge you again next time!"

Johan: "..."

Great, the "intelligence" level is still going up.

---

Lavaridge Town.

As the night deepened, a thin mist meandered through the starry sky, sometimes stretching across the distant veil, at other times forming elusive illusions, like the ever-changing Pokémon. The courtyard, with its winding paths, isolated the noise of the outside world, creating a serene atmosphere. In the backyard, an open-air hot spring, accompanied by the gentle sound of flowing water, stirred a ripple of tranquility in one's heart.

Johan, wrapped in a white bath towel around his waist, slowly stepped into the warm waters.

Steven was already lazily lounging in the hot spring, his arms stretched out as he leaned against the stone edge of the pool. A neatly folded towel rested atop his head.

In the end, Steven, Roxanne, and Flannery's plan to sneak into the mountains was thwarted by Johan's victory over Flannery, putting an end to their escape attempt.

As for soaking in the hot springs, that had always been part of the planned itinerary. However, it wasn't quite the mixed-gender bath Johan had imagined.

On one side of the pool where Johan and Steven soaked stood a wooden partition. On the other side, Roxanne and Flannery were enjoying the hot spring.

Splash, splash—

As the hot water reached his abdomen, Johan felt the "magic" of the hot spring as all his fatigue seemed to melt away.

The rising steam, mingling with the mist, outlined the vague, dreamlike image of a handsome youth bathing.

"Mr. Johan?"

Hearing the movement, Steven opened his eyes slowly, briefly dazed before quickly regaining his focus.

Johan leaned his head back against the smooth stones at the pool's edge, allowing his body to become buoyant in the water, feeling light and somewhat dizzy, as if floating on air.

He waved his hand dismissively, cutting off Steven's words.

"Steven, we're not that far apart in age, right?"

Hearing this, Steven paused, then nodded. "If I'm not mistaken, you're only two years older than me. You completed a four-year university program in just three years, finished the six-year advanced academy in five years, and as for the primary academy…"

Are you checking my background?

You know so much.

"So, when it's just us, there's no need to be so formal. Just call me by my name," Johan interrupted him again.

"...Johan?" Steven hesitated briefly before trying out the name.

"Hmm." Johan lazily hummed in response, eyes half-closed.

A teacher must learn to connect with students, to bridge gaps between them.

This was especially true for a psychologist and their "patients."

Of course, this wasn't influenced by Steven's status or the fact that he probably didn't even realize just how wealthy his family was. At least, that wasn't the main reason.

"By the way," Johan continued, "Lavaridge Town's hot springs are quite famous, aren't they? Why is it that this place, which seems pretty decent, is so empty with only us here... Don't tell me you bought the place?"

Hearing this, Steven chuckled and shook his head. "Not exactly."

But before Johan could speak again, Steven added, "The nearby properties are already owned by my family."

Johan: "..."

He raised his hand and gave a thumbs-up.

Well, that made things easier.

"Gastly," Johan called out.

Splash!

Suddenly, the once calm surface of the water erupted as Gastly shot up from the hot spring.

With a mischievous grin plastered on its face, Gastly declared, "Ka-kah!"

Gastly evolves into... Ghost-Damp?

Johan & Steven: "..."

But Johan quickly sat upright in alarm, "Wait, aren't you soaking in water? You're made of poisonous gas!"

"Ka-kah?"

Gastly: Σ(°ロ°)

After a brief episode of chaos...

Steven and Johan now sat on the edge of the pool, with Gastly sheepishly floating nearby.

Fortunately, since Gastly could control its poison, no harm was done despite being submerged in the water.

"Since we're at it, let's bring everyone out," Steven said, tossing a few Poké Balls into the air.

In an instant, his shiny Metagross, Lairon, Skarmory, and Claydol materialized.

However, Lairon and Claydol, being Rock- and Ground-type Pokémon, instinctively recoiled from the water, and as a bird Pokémon, Skarmory didn't seem too fond of soaking in a hot spring either.

In the end, only Metagross and Gastly joined them in the water.

But both Johan and Steven were slightly on edge, repeatedly warning their Pokémon.

Control the poison (or the electromagnetic field).

Finally, the hot spring grew calm once more.

"Groooooss," Metagross grumbled, apparently feeling little from the experience.

"Ka-kah?" (What? Look at this.)

Gastly dove back into the water, blowing bubbles with a "gurgle, gurgle."

Metagross glanced over at the Pokémon on the sidelines—Lairon and Skarmory.

Anyone wanna play with it?

Lairon and Skarmory simultaneously turned to Claydol.

Claydol: (ーー)

Seeing the Pokémon having a lively chat, Johan took the opportunity to relax. He leaned against the edge of the pool and pulled out his phone. Soon enough, he noticed a message from his client, "Eevee Eats Cabbage."

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: Alright, I've made the Pokéblocks. Feebas really likes them, and its condition seems to have improved a bit, though it's still shy around other Pokémon.

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: This may sound a bit forward, but could you help with some psychological therapy?

Psychological therapy?

If it were something like Mewtwo's case, it wouldn't be impossible, but for Feebas?

Was he supposed to have Feebas type?

Even video chats might not work—he'd probably need to use Hypnosis to conduct any psychological intervention.

[Grain in the Field]: Apologies, I have other work commitments, so...

A face-to-face meeting?

That's a bit risky.

Who knew what kind of person was really behind the cute username? For all he knew, it could be some creepy guy.

As someone who remembered their past life, Johan was very cautious about such things.

Since "Eevee Eats Cabbage" was offline, Johan's message didn't get an immediate response.

However, besides that, he received a message from another user.

[Number Wizard]: Poké Balls are a form of restraint on Pokémon, preventing them from achieving their full existence and happiness. I believe that Pokémon and humans should live separately—Pokémon in their own world, and humans behind the walls of their steel cities!

For some reason, seeing this message gave Johan a strange sense of déjà vu.

But then his thoughts returned to the question at hand.

Were captured Pokémon truly unhappy?

Johan glanced at the hot spring.

Floating on the surface, basking in the mist, was Gastly, who had fashioned a wooden tray to hold a bottle of MooMoo Milk. Several straws connected from the bottle directly to Gastly's mouth…

Was Gastly unhappy?

Nearby, Steven, wrapped in a towel, was scrubbing Lairon's back. Sparkling white suds covered his face as both he and Lairon wore matching smiles.

Metagross and Skarmory were waiting their turn.

Were they unhappy?

Not really.

Granted, there were indeed unhappy Pokémon in this world, but they were the exception rather than the rule.

'This is classic idealism, where someone's personal experience is projected onto everyone else, and they believe they need to build a "utopia" for all.'

Johan didn't reject this person's ideas outright, though. Their intentions were good—they wanted to protect Pokémon. It was just that their experiences had skewed their perspective.

Stubbornness.

Johan sighed.

A preconceived notion like this was the hardest to change.

Even if he presented the real "truth," if it didn't match what this person wanted, they would simply reject it.

So, the best approach in such cases was not to directly oppose them. If you did, they wouldn't listen to a word you said.

Instead, you had to see things from their point of view, stand where they stood, and earn their trust. Only then could you present them with the true "reality" and perhaps help them see their "ideal world" more clearly.

After thinking it over for a while, Johan finally responded.

[Grain in the Field]: If you come across Pokémon that are being mistreated, please do your best to save them. To those Pokémon, you might be the only light in their dark world—their hero.

After sending the message, Johan set his phone aside and sighed once more.

"Ka-kah?"

Noticing Johan's shift in mood, Gastly floated over, spitting out the straw it had been sipping from.

What's wrong?

Johan shook his head.

It was hard to imagine what someone must have gone through to develop such extreme ideas. His thoughts grew heavier.

"Ka-kah!"

The mist around Gastly shifted, transforming into a pair of clamshells, followed by sharp spikes extending out…

Gastly Cloyster mode!

"Pfft—"

Seeing Gastly perfectly imitate Cloyster, Johan couldn't help but burst out laughing.

"Ka-kah-kah~~"

Gastly joined in, laughing alongside him.

Though its laugh was a bit creepy.

Steven, drawn by the sound of their laughter, couldn't help but remark, "Johan, you and Gastly have such a great bond."

Boom!!

Suddenly, a loud explosion echoed, accompanied by the startled screams of Roxanne and Flannery.

---

---

---

# 27: The Change at Mt. Chimney

The dense velvet sky obscured the moon, and the lingering mist carried an unsettling chill, as if some unseen dark presence lurked in the shadows, watching. In contrast, the explosive roar that followed was like a harbinger of light, tearing through the dark curtain with a deafening crack. It rumbled on, echoing like the earth itself was splitting open.

Hearing the sound, both Johan and Steven's expressions changed, and Gastly along with Metagross immediately moved to their sides, ready to protect them.

The next second...

CRASH!!

A massive boulder came crashing down, shattering the wooden partition that separated the two hot springs.

The two springs were now connected.

"What happened?" Steven quickly asked.

"There's—" Roxanne started, but her face, already flushed from soaking in the hot water, now turned an even deeper shade of red, much like a ripe apple.

Her cheeks were burning, her usual twin tails had come undone, and her damp hair now draped over her shoulders. Fortunately, all four of them—Roxanne, Flannery, Johan, and Steven—were wrapped in towels.

Steven, too, seemed taken aback, but quickly realized the situation and displayed a hint of embarrassment.

"A rock! A huge rock just fell down from the mountain!" Flannery exclaimed, far less self-conscious than the others, and immediately filled in the missing details.

In fact, given her similar build to Johan and Steven... well, let's just say, ahem...

The mountain?

Johan, the only one who remained somewhat calm, furrowed his brow and looked up at the mountain.

Since Lavaridge Town's hot springs rely on the heat from Mt. Chimney, many of the town's inns and hot springs are located near the mountain.

Johan and Steven's side of the springs was relatively safe, but Roxanne and Flannery's side was almost directly adjacent to the rocky slopes of Mt. Chimney.

Because of the distance and the jagged terrain, it was hard to determine what had happened up on the mountain. However, the dark night made one thing visible—a faint glow of fire could be seen rising from the upper reaches of the mountain.

The flickering flames looked like a sword piercing the night sky, growing larger and brighter as if threatening to tear a hole through the darkness.

"Could it be that the volcano has opened up, and lava is starting to seep out?" Johan muttered to himself.

Upon hearing this, the expressions of the other three drastically changed.

Flannery, who had already changed into her clothes, stared intently at the firelight in the night sky, her face set with determination. It was clear that she had every intention of heading up the mountain.

If Mt. Chimney had indeed developed a fissure that allowed lava to escape, the position of the firelight suggested that Lavaridge Town could be in imminent danger.

Lavaridge Town was Flannery's home—there was no way she could sit back and do nothing!

It wasn't just her. Steven and Roxanne, though less visibly shaken, shared the same concern.

Noticing the determination in their faces, Johan sighed inwardly.

He understood that at this point, simply acting as their teacher wasn't enough to quell the sense of duty and justice burning within them.

Though the firelight wasn't overly intense yet, and the dark red hue of Mt. Chimney's surface often concealed such dangers, the fact that a boulder had fallen alerted them to the problem. The fire was growing, albeit slowly, indicating that if lava was leaking, now was the best time to intervene before things got worse.

There wasn't much time to deliberate, but Johan had already made his decision.

"Roxanne, Flannery, I want you two to notify the Lavaridge Gym, the local police, and the other teachers at the Pokémon Center. Steven and I will go take a look."

If anyone had to go investigate, it was only right that as the teacher, Johan should take the lead.

Steven, being the strongest among them, was also a necessary part of the team.

Additionally, his Metagross, Skarmory, and Claydol would be essential in case things got out of hand, providing them a way to escape if necessary.

"Mr. Johan, I—" Roxanne started to protest but was quickly silenced by Johan's firm, unyielding gaze.

"I understand," she finally said, lowering her head and taking a deep breath, realizing the gravity of the situation.

Flannery, however, wasn't as easy to convince.

"No! I have to go!" she demanded, and before Johan could even respond, she added, "I know the area best! I know a shortcut that'll get us there quickly!"

Time was of the essence. Johan could see the determination in Flannery's eyes and, after a moment, replied in a serious tone, "Alright. You lead the way, but if anything happens, you have to follow my commands."

"Deal!"

"Ka-kah!"

Gastly brought Johan his clothes.

Mt. Chimney.

Jagged rocks and steep cliffs surrounded them as three figures quickly made their way through the rugged terrain.

"This is the shortcut you were talking about?" Johan leaped over uneven rocks, keeping pace with Flannery.

"I used to take this path all the time! It might look dangerous, but as long as you don't misstep, it's perfectly safe," Flannery called back from the front.

As long as we don't misstep...

Both Steven and Johan glanced down at their feet.

Silence.

Johan felt a bit of regret.

If it had just been him and Steven, they could've used Skarmory, Metagross, and Claydol to fly straight up to their destination.

While relying on Psychic-type abilities to fly that high would've drained a lot of energy from their Pokémon, this route did conserve their strength in case of emergencies, and it wasn't much slower. Still... it was a bit of a strain on those who weren't built like "super rookies."

Fortunately, despite Flannery's impulsive nature, her shortcut did seem to be leading them directly to their destination.

Soon enough, they reached a small plateau.

"Hicha, hicha—"

Scattered across the plateau were several Geodude and even some Graveler, all huddled in the corners, trembling in fear.

As Johan, Steven, and Flannery approached, the Pokémon retreated even further, causing small rocks to tumble down the mountainside.

However, the trio's attention was immediately drawn to the sight before them—a glowing red crack in the rocky wall of the mountain.

The fissure wasn't large, barely wide enough for a person to pass through, but the firelight they had seen from below was emanating from within it.

"What is this...?"

The trio moved closer, feeling the intense heat radiating from the crack. It was far hotter than the warmth they had felt back in Lavaridge Town.

Flap, flap—

Suddenly, a Zubat darted out of the crack, flying frantically away from the mountain.

Johan, Steven, and Flannery quickly stepped aside to avoid any collision.

Moments later, a swarm of Zubat, along with a few Golbat, poured out of the fissure, hastily fleeing from Mt. Chimney.

Seeing this, the three trainers' expressions grew more solemn.

Wild Pokémon are always the first to sense changes in their environment. Both the Geodude and the fleeing Zubat indicated that something inside the mountain was terrifying them—or perhaps it was something even more dangerous.

"Gastly," Johan called quietly.

Given the circumstances, rushing in blindly was not an option. Johan held Flannery back, preventing her from charging in recklessly.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gastly floated out from Johan's jacket, peering into the dark fissure, clearly unafraid—if anything, it seemed slightly excited.

"Be careful."

Johan couldn't help but give a stern warning.

"Ka-kah!"

Gastly blinked at Johan and let out a mischievous laugh before transforming into a dark cloud of mist, disappearing into the fissure.

Time seemed to stretch on, each second ticking by slowly.

Although Johan usually maintained his composure, he couldn't help but feel a little anxious this time—not for himself, but for Gastly.

After all, the situation inside the mountain was unknown, and Gastly's strength wasn't exactly top-tier.

Moreover, this was Mt. Chimney.

"Ka-kah~~"

Thankfully, Gastly soon emerged from the fissure.

It was panting heavily, tongue hanging out, clearly displeased with the intense heat inside the mountain.

But seeing Gastly return brought a wave of relief to Johan.

"How is it, Gastly?"

"Ka-kah!!"

Gastly inhaled deeply, causing its body to puff up.

Inside... there's something BIG!

---

---

---

# 28: Warning! Warning!

A "big one"?

Johan's face tightened.

That phrase could mean many things.

Especially inside Mt. Chimney, Johan instinctively thought of Groudon—the terrifying entity said to be slumbering deep within the volcano.

But on second thought, it didn't seem likely.

If it really were Groudon, with the power it holds, Mt. Chimney would have already erupted by now. There would be far more destruction than just this small fissure.

Is there no more information to go on?

Johan glanced again at Gastly.

After today, he decided that as soon as they got back, he would begin teaching Gastly how to recognize other Pokémon.

At the very least, it needed to be able to identify different Pokémon. Otherwise, such vague reports could continue to be a problem in the future.

"Ka-kah..."

Gastly seemed to realize that its description wasn't clear enough. It narrowed its eyes in thought, its dark mist swirling around it.

Suddenly, Gastly seemed to have a breakthrough. The black mist shifted and stretched, slowly forming limbs as it dropped to the ground. Then it opened its mouth wide, exhaling a puff of dark smoke.

Limbs?

"A Camerupt?" Steven guessed.

But Camerupt, though large, wouldn't exactly qualify as a "big one," would it?

Johan considered another possibility.

Could it be... the legendary Pokémon, Heatran?

Although Mt. Coronet in Sinnoh was typically where he would expect to find a Heatran, theoretically, Mt. Chimney belonged to Groudon's domain, and a Heatran wouldn't dare claim it as a habitat.

But it wasn't entirely out of the question.

After all, Groudon was dormant, and Mt. Chimney's high-temperature environment would certainly appeal to a Heatran...

If it was Heatran, though, that would spell serious trouble.

Boom!!

Before Johan could ponder further, the sound of rocks shattering echoed from the fissure.

Immediately after, the narrow crack, already radiating scorching heat, belched forth another wave of intense warmth. A bright, glowing stream of molten lava started to trickle from the corner of the fissure.

Lava!

"Geodude!"

Seeing the lava, the Geodude and Graveler already huddled in the corner became even more frantic.

At the same time, the lava began to slowly snake its way down the mountainside.

"Flannery!" Johan called out sharply.

"On it!" Flannery quickly took out two Poké Balls. In a flash of red light, she summoned a Numel and a Slugma.

Slugma, a Lava Pokémon, had a body resembling molten magma. It thrived in volcanic environments and felt right at home in such high temperatures. While the lava would be deadly to most Pokémon, to Slugma, it was like a comfortable bed.

The Slugma slithered forward, intercepting the thin flow of lava, blocking its path down the mountain.

Numel joined Slugma in trying to control the flow.

"Johan, Slugma can't hold this back for long!" Flannery called out urgently.

"Let's move!" Johan ordered.

Despite his growing concern, Johan knew they had no choice.

The lava had already started to flow. If they didn't act quickly, the lava could spill further, leading to a massive eruption of molten rock from the heart of Mt. Chimney.

And if it really turned out to be Heatran...

Well, they'd have to do their best to either calm it down or buy enough time.

Roxanne had already gone to alert the authorities in Lavaridge Town, including Flannery's grandfather—the retired former Elite Four member. He should be able to handle Heatran...

As long as they could get there in time.

There was another reason why Johan decided to proceed.

That reason was Gastly.

Despite the urgency of the situation, Gastly's report, although unclear, suggested that if the danger were too great, it would have warned them to stay back.

Johan trusted Gastly completely.

"I'll go first!" Steven declared, summoning Claydol to his side as he entered the fissure before anyone else.

The narrow crack made it difficult for Metagross or Skarmory to move, so the smaller, more maneuverable Claydol was the perfect choice.

Johan, with Gastly by his side, quickly followed behind.

If the heat from outside had been uncomfortable, it was nothing compared to the suffocating warmth inside the fissure. The air was so thick with heat that both Johan and Steven were drenched in sweat almost immediately.

Surprisingly, the temperature wasn't as unbearable as they had expected. After all, Zubat and Golbat had been living inside, so the internal climate wasn't completely inhospitable.

Guided by Gastly, they ventured deeper into the tunnel, the heat intensifying with every step, drenching their clothes and painting their faces with a fiery red glow.

Boom! Boom!

As they moved further inside, a series of deep, rumbling sounds began to echo through the tunnel.

Finally, they emerged into a larger, open space within the volcano.

Scanning their surroundings, they quickly spotted several Numel huddled together in one corner. Known for their docile demeanor, even the Numel looked terrified—eyes wide with fear and uncertainty.

What were they so afraid of?

Following their gaze, Johan and Steven finally saw the source of the disturbance.

A Camerupt!

The massive Pokémon stood before them, covered in bright orange fur, with two large rocky humps on its back. From those humps spewed molten lava, like a miniature volcano erupting on its own.

It was clearly agitated. Its body was trembling, and it stomped its feet violently, causing the ground around it to crack and crumble. Behind it, a wall of the cavern had been scorched black, with fresh, molten lava slowly leaking from a jagged crack.

It was obvious now—the small stream of lava they had seen earlier had originated from this Camerupt.

However, both Johan and Steven were taken aback by one detail.

Typically, a Camerupt stood around 1.8 to 2 meters tall. But the one standing before them now was at least 3 meters tall!

This was a giant Camerupt!

The "big one" that Gastly had mentioned.

Boom! Boom!

Two more spurts of molten lava erupted from the volcano-like humps on Camerupt's back.

As Johan and Steven locked eyes with it, its bloodshot eyes glared back at them!

"Roooaaaar!!"

The Camerupt let out a furious roar, its open mouth glowing with intense heat.

Johan and Steven's expressions shifted.

Steven quickly reached for more Poké Balls, summoning Metagross, Skarmory, and Lairon to the field.

But all three of his Pokémon were Steel-types, which were naturally at a disadvantage against Camerupt's Fire and Ground-type moves. Moreover, this was Camerupt's home turf.

At the same time, Johan's vision filled with Camerupt's Pokédex information.

[Camerupt]

Type: Fire/Ground

Ability: Magma Armor

Moves: Rock Slide, Fissure, Flamethrower, Growl, Tackle, Ember, Focus Energy, Stomp, Lava Plume, Amnesia, Smokescreen, Earth Power, Take Down

Emotion: Furious (It may have consumed something it shouldn't have.)

Status: Unstable (This isn't a normal state for a Pokémon. WARNING! WARNING!)

Friendship: -50 (Danger! You stepped forward with your left foot first!)

This Camerupt was no joke!

Even if it weren't in this frenzied state, it would still be leagues stronger than Gastly.

The frenzy had increased its strength, but it also made its already low intelligence even worse.

Johan exchanged a glance with Steven.

BOOM!!

A surge of fire exploded toward them.

"Metagross, Claydol, use Psychic!"

"Gastly, use Curse!"

Their commands came almost simultaneously.

Bzzz—

A transparent, light blue psychic barrier formed instantly, blocking the raging flames just three meters away from Steven and Johan.

"Ka-kah—"

Gastly's body swelled momentarily as an eerie force invisibly burrowed into Camerupt's body, planting the seeds of a curse that would soon take root.

"Skarmory, use Sand Attack! Lairon, use Mud-Slap!"

The situation with Camerupt, their Pokémon types, and the sheer weight of their collective experience led both trainers to adopt the same strategy—they would play it safe and wear Camerupt down.

Dust and mud flew, obscuring Camerupt's vision as it unleashed another torrent of fire, scorching the cavern.

Rocks began to fall, and though the fire started to die down, Camerupt's rage only seemed to grow.

It took a deep breath, and a white energy field surrounded its body—Focus Energy!

Then, it reared up on its hind legs, gathering earth-toned energy between its front hooves.

This is bad!

It's preparing to use Fissure!

Fissure is a Ground-type move that, under normal circumstances, guarantees a knockout if it hits!

While most of the Pokémon present, including Gastly, wouldn't be too affected by it, Johan feared what the aftermath of this move could bring.

One wrong step and this entire cavern might collapse...

---

---

---

# 29: Heatran!

BOOM!!

The ground split open with a deafening crack, sending rocks flying in all directions. A massive chasm appeared, as if the earth itself had opened its mouth, threatening to swallow everything.

Camerupt's "Fissure" had landed.

In a situation like this, where there wasn't an overwhelming difference in strength, no one would let their Pokémon stand still and get hit by such a devastating move.

The sheer force of the attack made Johan feel as if the entire mountain was shaking violently.

The only silver lining was that despite the power of the Fissure, Johan's and Steven's Pokémon were unharmed.

Johan and Steven released their grip on Metagross, landing back on solid ground.

The fact was, the enraged Camerupt, with its vision obscured, couldn't properly target Gastly and the others.

But things couldn't continue like this.

Who knew if Camerupt's next Fissure wouldn't cause a further collapse, worsening the lava flow from Mt. Chimney.

The problem was that even though both Johan and Steven wanted to speed things up and subdue Camerupt, it wasn't that simple.

With Camerupt in its current state, combined with the power gap between it and Gastly, Johan knew that status-altering moves likely wouldn't have much effect.

Still, he had to try.

"Gastly, use Haze!"

If they were lucky and could poison Camerupt, combined with the Curse already in place and Steven's Pokémon, the tide of the battle could shift.

"Ka-kah…"

Gastly sighed inwardly.

This was tough.

This Camerupt was clearly way out of Gastly's league. Relying on it to wear down Camerupt was a long shot.

But Gastly wasn't one to back down from a challenge. "Why not try? There doesn't seem to be a better plan."

"Johan, try this."

Steven tossed something toward Johan—a dark, purplish-black sphere, encased in a thin layer of transparent material.

Huh?

Johan caught it and glanced down.

A Toxic Orb?!

A Steel-type specialist carrying around a Toxic Orb?

This thing wasn't cheap, either.

[Toxic Orb: A ball filled with potent poison gas. Holding it puts the user in a "badly poisoned" state (WARNING: Do not consume!)

But now wasn't the time to dwell on such details.

Clutching the Toxic Orb tightly, Johan formed a bold idea in his mind.

"Ka-kah?"

Gastly, also noticing the Toxic Orb, had its eyes light up, clearly intrigued.

"Ka-kah!!"

Johan threw the orb toward Gastly, but before Gastly could celebrate, Johan said, "Hold on to it for now. Our goal is to get it to Camerupt!"

"Ka-kah?!" Gastly's voice rose in surprise.

"Don't worry, we'll take it back after Camerupt goes down," Johan reassured.

Steven, overhearing, seemed to realize Johan's plan.

"Of course!" he muttered under his breath.

"Steven, keep it distracted. Gastly and I will make our move," Johan ordered.

"Got it!"

Meanwhile, Camerupt showed no signs of calming down. Its back-mounted volcano continued to erupt with lava, and flames began to build in its mouth.

It was readying another attack!

Johan and Steven both thought the same thing.

Camerupt suddenly lifted its head and launched a fiery blast upward. The flames formed a glowing fireball in the air before exploding, sending smaller bursts of flame flying in every direction!

Lava Plume!

Now's our chance!

Metagross and Claydol quickly activated their psychic barriers, doing their best to block the incoming fire.

Meanwhile, Skarmory flapped its wings in midair, causing a sharp, ear-piercing screech with its Metal Sound, drawing Camerupt's attention.

Lairon, having been resummoned, stomped the ground, sending two rocky chains shooting up, wrapping around Camerupt and temporarily restraining its movement.

"Gastly!" Johan shouted.

"Ka-kah!"

Suddenly, from below the ground, Gastly emerged right in front of Camerupt's face, hurling the Toxic Orb straight into Camerupt's mouth.

Gulp!

Camerupt swallowed it with a loud gulp.

Before it could even register what had happened, a purple glow began to spread across its body.

Camerupt is now badly poisoned!

Its body wobbled, its vision becoming blurry, and its limbs felt weak.

The frenzied state that had been pushing its body to the limit caused the poison to spread even faster.

On top of that, Camerupt had already been fighting for some time, its body nearing exhaustion. The poison was only accelerating that process.

Camerupt shook its head in an attempt to clear its vision, but the purple hue only deepened, and it began to stagger.

It tried to mount one final attack.

But its efforts were in vain.

At last, in front of Johan and Steven, Camerupt collapsed heavily onto the ground.

Johan and Steven exchanged glances, both letting out a sigh of relief.

Now they could focus on finding a way to seal the lava fissure.

Under Johan's direction, Gastly began scouting the rock layers, searching for the optimal spot to reinforce and block the lava flow.

But just when they thought the situation was under control, an unexpected development occurred.

Boom, boom, boom—

Heavy footsteps echoed from a different tunnel, followed by an intense wave of heat, as if a surge of molten lava was rushing toward them.

Johan and Steven's faces both darkened as they turned toward the tunnel, which was now glowing with an ominous red light.

When Johan's eyes adjusted, his pupils constricted.

Emerging from the shadows was a hulking figure, its reddish-brown body appearing as though it was forged from molten lava and steel. Its powerful limbs, glinting with a metallic sheen, allowed it to cling to the cave walls almost as if it were walking upside down.

Its fiery, molten eyes locked onto Johan and Steven.

An even bigger threat?

Sensing the danger, Steven's Pokémon immediately gathered to defend him.

"Wait!" Johan quickly stopped Steven, his voice low.

"Don't move."

Steven understood the gravity of the situation, calming his Pokémon as he quietly asked, "Is that...?"

The pressure emanating from the creature was overwhelming, like a tidal wave of molten rock bearing down on them. Steven knew immediately that this was no ordinary opponent.

This wasn't just a matter of a difference in strength.

Johan's voice dropped to almost a whisper as he replied, "The legendary beast born from magma..."

"Heatran, the guardian of volcanoes."

"Heatran... the legendary beast?" Steven was taken aback, his eyes glimmering with awe.

As a fan of Steel-type Pokémon, Steven knew all too well what it meant to face a Fire/Steel Pokémon like Heatran.

With five 4x resistances and four 2x resistances, Heatran was a fortress of defense!

Johan, for his part, was quite familiar with Heatran as well.

Before Pokémon Sword and Shield was released, Heatran had been a staple in competitive battling. Its Fire/Steel typing gave it incredible resistances, and while it had a glaring 4x weakness to Ground, items like Air Balloon could help mitigate that issue.

And both of Heatran's abilities—Flash Fire and Flame Body—were top-notch.

But as more powerful Fire-type Pokémon were introduced, Heatran gradually faded from prominence, especially with the advent of moves like Scorching Sands, which pushed it out of the top tier.

Still, that was the Heatran from Johan's previous life in the games.

What stood before them now was the very real and very imposing Heatran, a mythical creature with immense power.

However...

After staring them down for a while, Heatran made no move to attack.

In fact...

Heatran waved a claw at them.

"Is it... telling us to leave?" Steven looked at Johan, unsure.

Is Heatran protecting Mt. Chimney? Johan wondered.

Because after Heatran's appearance, the lava flow seemed to be stabilizing.

"Gastly," Johan called out, "let's go!"

"Ka-kah!" Gastly's voice echoed from the lava fissure.

"There's a weird red stone here!"

Peering through the crack, Johan could just make out Gastly flying in circles above the lava, as if it had discovered something.

Steven's eyes lit up.

A stone?

But both Johan and Heatran's eyes widened in alarm.

"Get away from that stone, now!"

"Ka-kah?"

---

---

---

# 30: Former Elite Four Member

"Ka-kah?"

Though confused, Gastly, sensing the tone in Johan's voice, obediently returned to his side.

Seeing this, Heatran also relaxed slightly.

"From now on, stay away from rocks in lava, especially the red ones," Johan instructed. "And if they have stripes on them, don't even think about flying near them."

After thinking for a moment, he added, "But if you accidentally do get close, stop floating around like that."

"Ka-kah?"

Gastly blinked a couple of times.

Striped red stones? What could be so special about them?

In reality, that stone wasn't all that unique, but if one looked closely, they'd notice that a significant amount of the lava was seeping through the cracks around it.

Obviously, the stone was helping to block the lava flow. If Gastly had accidentally loosened it, the outcome could've been disastrous.

From the way Heatran had reacted, it was likely that it had placed the stone there itself, though for what exact reason was unknown.

Perhaps it was to protect the Pokémon living in the mountain and below?

Or maybe there was something within the lava that needed to be kept in check?

"Roar—"

Heatran crawled out of the tunnel, hanging upside down from the cave ceiling. It waved its claw at Johan and Steven again, signaling for them to leave and not cause any more trouble.

Johan, for his part, was more than ready to leave.

While Heatran hadn't been hostile, who could say how long its goodwill would last?

Johan retrieved the unconscious Camerupt into a Pokéball.

Taming this Camerupt would be no easy task. Just healing it would likely take a long time and be quite expensive. For now, it was more practical to store it in a Pokéball.

With Heatran watching them closely, Johan and Steven slowly retreated, taking their Pokémon along.

However, just before leaving the cave, Johan hesitated briefly before taking out three bottles of Moomoo Milk and some energy cubes.

Facing the somewhat surprised Heatran, Johan said, "Thanks."

"Roar?"

After the two left, Heatran slowly approached the items Johan had left behind, sniffing them cautiously. Its eyes lit up.

It raised its head and glanced in the direction they had gone.

These people... aren't bad, Heatran thought. Picking up the goods, it returned deeper into the cave, giving the red-hot stone a gentle pat and letting out a sigh.

That little one has sharp instincts...

As Johan and Steven emerged from the crack in the mountain, both let out long sighs of relief. The hot, stifling air receded, and as the cooler outside breeze hit their sweat-soaked clothes, they felt a bit of a chill.

"Ka-kah, ka-kah—"

Gastly was also taking deep breaths, clearly relieved to be out of the suffocating heat of Mt. Chimney.

"Looks like the issue's been resolved."

A calm voice came from nearby.

Only then did Johan and Steven notice Flannery standing with an older man, his hair gray but his posture tall and imposing. He wore round-framed glasses and looked to be in excellent shape.

Flannery caught their gaze and winked playfully.

"Master Mura?" Steven said, testingly, as if recognizing the old man.

The old man nodded seriously, "I'm no longer part of the Elite Four. I'm now the Gym Leader of Lavaridge Town."

Johan and Steven exchanged a glance, neither betraying much emotion.

"Gym Leader Mura," they both greeted respectfully.

"Hmm." Mura nodded, clearly satisfied, though a faint smile tugged at the corners of his mouth.

"Grandpa, your smile is showing," Flannery interrupted, teasingly.

"Didn't I tell you not to go running up Mt. Chimney whenever you feel like it?" Mura's voice suddenly rose, launching into a stern lecture. "It's dangerous, especially taking those treacherous paths!"

Mura's scolding seemed endless, but Flannery remained unfazed, standing beside him with a bored expression and even casually picking at her ear.

"Ka-kah?"

Gastly floated near Johan's head.

This guy was an Elite Four member? It doesn't seem like much.

Johan shot Gastly a sidelong glance, then turned his attention back to Mura, who had stopped lecturing his granddaughter. His expression shifted when Flannery muttered, "Dad used to take me here all the time."

Mura's face showed a complex range of emotions, but he quickly turned his attention to Johan.

"You must be Teacher Johan, correct? Thank you for taking care of Flannery."

To Johan's surprise, the former Elite Four member and current Gym Leader bowed deeply, a full ninety degrees.

Johan quickly stepped forward, helping Mura up. "You're too kind, Gym Leader Mura. I'm just doing what a teacher should do."

Mura sighed. "You wouldn't understand. Flannery's been a handful since she was a child..."

Mura then began recounting Flannery's childhood stories with surprising enthusiasm.

Like the time she accidentally got her hair singed by a Slugma, or when she sat on the back of a Torkoal and burned herself.

"Grandpa!"

Embarrassed, Flannery's face turned beet red. She immediately dashed down the mountain, leaving the rest of them behind.

"Flannery, get back here! Grandpa's not done talking!" Mura shouted after her, clearly concerned, and hurried to chase her.

"Ka-kah?" Gastly floated near Johan.

They're kind of alike, huh?

Kind of? They're exactly the same, Johan thought with a wry smile.

In Lavaridge Gym, Mura, Officer Jenny, Nurse Joy, and Johan's group were gathered in the reception room.

After listening to Johan and Steven's account of the events, Mura seemed a bit surprised but also as though he had expected it.

"So you encountered Heatran?" Mura asked.

"What's a Heatran?" Flannery interrupted.

"Did you know about that Heatran, Gym Leader Mura?" Johan asked in return.

He had suspected as much. Heatran's lack of hostility and even faint kindness toward them had suggested that it had prior interactions with someone from the town—likely Mura.

"What is a Heatran?" Flannery asked again, louder this time.

"Let's just say we've crossed paths before. Heatran is the guardian of Mt. Chimney," Mura explained, taking a deep breath before sipping his tea. He didn't seem eager to elaborate further.

"That explains it." Johan nodded, also sipping his tea.

He had no interest in pressing the matter.

"But seriously, what's a Heatran?" Flannery's frustration was growing as she pulled at her hair.

Steven chuckled softly and explained, "It's a mythical Pokémon—Heatran, the Lava Dome Pokémon."

Flannery's eyes lit up at the mention of a mythical Pokémon.

Meanwhile, Nurse Joy and Officer Jenny had completed their initial treatment and examination of the weakened Camerupt.

"Excuse me, but I'll need to take this one back to the Pokémon Center for further care," Nurse Joy said gently, her voice soft and soothing. The Chansey by her side nodded in agreement.

"We'll also investigate the cause of Camerupt's rampage," Officer Jenny added. "Thank you for your assistance. A formal letter of thanks will be sent to your academy in a few days."

Compared to Nurse Joy's warmth, Officer Jenny was much more serious.

As the two prepared to leave, Johan hesitated briefly before speaking.

"Officer Jenny, I have a hunch—perhaps this Camerupt might be linked to the giant Graveler that was found outside Mauville City."

"Oh?" Officer Jenny looked intrigued. "Do you have additional information, Teacher Johan?"

Johan shook his head. "It's just a theory. I hope it doesn't interfere with your investigation."

Officer Jenny gave him a deep look before nodding. "We'll coordinate with the Mauville City Police for a possible information exchange."

That night, in a twin room on the third floor of the Lavaridge Town Pokémon Center:

"Here you go." Johan tossed a small, purple sphere to Gastly.

It was the Toxic Orb.

"Ka-kah!" Gastly's eyes lit up, and it caught the orb in its mouth, eagerly licking the surface with its tongue.

It could feel that this Toxic Orb would greatly enhance its Poison-type abilities!

However, after licking for a while, Gastly suddenly paused.

"Ka-kah?!"

Wait, how did they even get this back?

Hearing Gastly's question, Johan nonchalantly replied, "Oh, Nurse Joy used a machine to retrieve it."

Gastly stared at him, but couldn't find any sign of deceit in Johan's expression.

Is that true?

But then Gastly noticed Johan removing a pair of white gloves and tossing them into the trash.

Gastly froze.

A second later:

"Ka-kah!!"

I don't need this tongue anymore!
