# 48: Instructional Battle

Rental Battle.

A rental battle involves using borrowed Pokémon instead of your own. It's an essential test for trainers.

After all, commanding someone else's Pokémon is quite challenging. Not knowing the Pokémon's abilities or moves requires trainers to be knowledgeable and adaptable. It's also a test of their quick thinking and ability to strategize.

In fact, rental battles are not uncommon in the Pokémon world.

Battle Factory, Battle Tent, Battle Tower—all have similar formats. In Pokémon Adventures: Emerald Chapter, one of the protagonists, Greta, is known to be highly skilled in rental battles and has an impressive 100-win streak.

It's not just trainers; Rangers also rely on wild Pokémon, which is a similar concept.

[Skarmory]

Type: Steel, Flying

Ability: Keen Eye

Known Moves: Wing Attack, Slash, Steel Wing, Payback, Drill Peck, Metal Sound, Spikes, Pluck, Stealth Rock, Whirlwind, Substitute, Roost, Taunt, Iron Defense, Protect, Toxic…

Mood: Excited, Anxious (Will this really work?)

Condition: Excellent (Ready for battle)

Friendship: 50 (We bathed together, after all)

This is the information Johan could see about Steven's Skarmory.

Steven really trained it well, especially with the sheer number of moves—evidence of his "money power."

Some of the moves included in Johan's strategy were already known by Skarmory and at a decent level of mastery. Johan had simply added a few more into the mix.

This preparation was part of what gave Johan confidence going into the battle.

During the time Lance was busy getting Aerodactyl back into battle shape, Johan took the opportunity to put Skarmory through a quick, one-hour intensive crash course.

It was to establish a basic sense of strategy and understanding between them for smoother coordination in the battle.

Of course…

This bond couldn't compare to what Steven shared with his Skarmory.

However, Lance's bond with Aerodactyl wasn't exactly flawless either.

Plus, Aerodactyl had just suffered a loss. Even if it had been healed up, it was far from being in peak condition without proper treatment at a Pokémon Center.

Not to mention, Skarmory had a type advantage.

All in all…

"The advantage is mine."

Nearby, Steven and Metagross had taken Johan's previous spot on the bench. Unashamedly, Steven reached into Gastly's popcorn bucket, grabbed a handful, and shared some with Metagross.

Crunch, crunch—

"Hmm? Why does this popcorn taste like sunflower seeds?"

"Ka-kah!" Stare!

Gastly glared at Steven's hand, its face full of discontent.

But, considering that Steven was Johan's student…

Just this once, it decided to forgive him. Definitely not because Metagross was watching…

"Hey, do you think Johan will win?" Steven asked.

"Ka-kah~~" Gastly shook its head. No idea.

Steven's expression twitched.

"Then again, Johan doesn't usually do things he's not confident about," Steven mused.

"Ka-kah~~" Gastly nodded in agreement.

At that moment, they saw Johan walk onto the battlefield and raise a single finger.

I'm only going to demonstrate this once.

Steven's face immediately turned serious.

He had always believed that the strength Johan showed wasn't just what he revealed on the surface. This feeling only grew stronger the more they interacted.

Especially when Johan handed him that strategy manual for Skarmory.

Johan, just how deeply have you hidden your strength?

On the battlefield, Lance had finished calming down Aerodactyl and was getting serious.

Anyone who earned praise from Drake was undoubtedly not to be underestimated.

While his bond with Aerodactyl wasn't at its peak, it was surely stronger than the bond Johan had with Steven's Skarmory.

Johan had also seen Aerodactyl battle against Metagross earlier. If he was still willing to borrow someone else's Pokémon for a fight, it meant he had confidence in his strategy.

Although the goal of the battle was to help Aerodactyl recover, as a trainer, Lance was getting excited.

He longed to face strong opponents. Both Steven and Johan were formidable foes who demanded his full attention and concentration.

"Kaa~~"

Skarmory flapped its wings, eyes gleaming sharply.

"Don't worry," Johan reassured it, gently stroking its metallic feathers.

"If we lose, it's my fault. If we win, it's all thanks to you. No pressure—just give it your best."

"Kaa!!"

Skarmory's respect for Johan grew even more.

"Begin!"

At Steven's signal, both Johan and Lance focused their attention on the battle.

"Aerodactyl, Stone Edge!" Lance commanded, sweeping aside his cape dramatically.

This time, instead of opening with Dragon Dance, he decided to take the offensive immediately.

Changing tactics?

Well, no matter how he changes it…

"Skarmory, block with Stealth Rock!"

Aerodactyl's body radiated an earthy glow, and sharp rocks seemed to sprout from its body. With a powerful flap of its wings, it sent the stone blades flying at Skarmory.

At the same time, Skarmory's eyes flashed yellow, and rocks also began to hover in midair before fading from sight, as if erased.

Aerodactyl's Stone Edge collided with these invisible rocks, producing a series of explosive sounds.

Using Stealth Rock for defense? Lance was taken aback.

Still, some of the stones pierced through and headed towards Skarmory.

"Take them head-on and use Iron Defense!" Johan's voice was calm.

His demeanor helped steady Skarmory's nerves.

Flapping its wings, Skarmory closed them around its body, blocking the incoming stones while its metallic feathers gleamed brightly.

Iron Defense greatly boosted its physical defense.

For a physically durable Pokémon like Skarmory, this was a substantial upgrade, creating pressure on Aerodactyl, which relied heavily on physical attacks.

"Aerodactyl, get in close with Thunder Fang!"

"Toxic!"

Speed-wise, Skarmory was no match for Aerodactyl, but the Stealth Rocks previously laid out served as obstacles.

In a one-on-one scenario, the typical "Spikes and Whirlwind" strategy wouldn't work.

After all, that strategy functions better in turn-based games, which differ significantly from real-world battles.

It could be borrowed but not copied exactly.

For example, Johan cleverly utilized Stealth Rock as both a shield and a hindrance to Aerodactyl's mobility.

Bzzzt!!

Golden electricity surged across Skarmory's body, dealing considerable damage.

Yet, the virulent toxins also seeped into Aerodactyl, causing its skin to take on a faint purple hue.

It had been poisoned with Toxic!

Lance's face darkened.

He realized he had to finish the fight quickly.

"Alright, Aerodactyl, use Fire Fang!"

This time, unlike the previous matchup with Metagross, where Steven had successfully exploited his advantage, Lance's aggressive tactics brought Aerodactyl closer, seizing the initiative.

"Skarmory, Substitute!"

Boom!!

Flames erupted, but they only burned through Skarmory's decoy feathers.

Lance had a sinking feeling in his gut.

The poison's effects were becoming increasingly evident on Aerodactyl.

However, he couldn't afford to panic, or he would lose focus.

"Aerodactyl, use Supersonic—"

"Taunt!"

Steven's eyes widened. "Did Johan 'read' Lance's move?"

Luck? Coincidence? Or a true prediction?

Aerodactyl's Supersonic was forcefully interrupted, causing its eyes to redden in frustration.

Yet Lance, being Lance, didn't lose his cool. He quickly adjusted his strategy. "Aerodactyl, Dragon Breath!"

Switching from Supersonic to Dragon Breath?

Impressive adaptation, Lance, Johan silently admired.

The rapid change in tactics demonstrated an increasing level of synergy between Lance and Aerodactyl.

"Skarmory, Protect!"

"Kaa!!"

A transparent green barrier appeared.

Bang!

The Protect move, however, has diminishing returns if used repeatedly, so even though Aerodactyl was temporarily blocked, it had to continue attacking.

"Fire Fang!"

"Iron Defense!"

Another head-on clash—though it strained Skarmory, the double boost from Iron Defense made it bearable.

Not to mention, Aerodactyl's attacks weren't even its STAB (Same Type Attack Bonus) moves, allowing Skarmory to survive.

"Aerodactyl, Thunder Fang!"

"Skarmory, Protect!"

Lance: "…"

Defend, defend, defend with Protect and Toxic stalling until the opponent implodes.

Gastly partially covered its eyes.

It couldn't bear to watch.

But it still peeked through its fingers, eyes sparkling.

This is… the magic of the "Protect Toxic Substitute" combo?

Johan is demonstrating it for me!

Steven also wore a complex expression, both awed and conflicted by what he was witnessing. Yet, the gleam in his eyes was unmistakable.

If Johan had merely cracked open a door to a new world for him before…

Today, he kicked it wide open!

At this point, the effects of Toxic were taking their toll, and Aerodactyl was on the verge of losing control.

"Aerodactyl, hold on! Skarmory isn't in good shape either—finish it with Fire Fang!"

Lance's voice was filled with determination as a green aura seemed to envelop him.

"Roar!!"

Aerodactyl let out a fierce roar and managed to regain its composure.

Flames danced on its razor-sharp fangs as it lunged at Skarmory.

"Roost."

Johan's calm command reached Skarmory.

"Roost?" Gastly and Steven's jaws dropped in sync.

Critical hit.

That's a psychological crit, they could almost hear the mental blow to Aerodactyl and Lance.

Skarmory touched down, recovering its stamina.

Thanks to its double Iron Defense boost, it withstood Aerodactyl's flames yet again.

But…

"Ka-kah!"

That's so cheap, so ruthless, and I love it!

Gastly was ecstatic.

With another round of Protect-Iron Defense-Protect, Aerodactyl's condition deteriorated rapidly, and its stamina reached rock bottom.

"Ahh…" Johan sighed. "Let's not drag this out any longer."

"Skarmory, finish with Brave Bird!"

Brave Bird—a move that deals heavy damage in exchange for recoil.

Skarmory's massive defense stat, boosted thrice by Iron Defense, was maxed out.

Boom!

Aerodactyl crashed heavily to the ground, fully knocked out.

Letting it fall due to poison would've been disrespectful.

In this demonstration, Johan combined the "Protect-Toxic-Substitute" strategy with the "Iron Defense Brave Bird" combo, skillfully blending in techniques from the "Spikes and Whirlwind" method—making this "lesson" as thorough as could be.

For some reason…

Lance seemed almost relieved after losing.

# 49: Keep a Low Profile

"We lost."

Lance accepted the outcome graciously.

He recalled the unconscious Aerodactyl, replaying the battle in his mind.

With every replay, a chill ran down his spine.

No, let's just stick to replaying the battle against Steven.

Replaying the one against Johan gave him an unsettling feeling.

And he made up his mind.

Once he got back, every one of his Pokémon had to learn Taunt! There was no other way to counter it.

"No wonder Drake praises this person so highly—using another trainer's Pokémon and still pulling off such tactics. This level of strategic prowess probably surpasses most regular Gym Leaders…"

After all, he was commanding someone else's Pokémon.

He didn't know Skarmory's characteristics or the proficiency of its moves.

The difficulty of managing that far exceeded commanding one's own Pokémon.

Moreover…

Steven's Skarmory was strong yet still willing to obey Johan, which spoke volumes about Johan's ability.

To Lance, Gastly seemed to be what was holding back Johan's full potential.

"But Johan has trained his Gastly extremely well. With Agatha's 'Terror Handbook' in hand, his training progress is bound to be swift."

Lance, blessed with Viridian Forest's power, the so-called "Viridian Power," could sense things to some degree, even if his control was far from perfect.

Johan's future strength...

"It's hard to say."

"At least, he could reach the level of an Elite Four."

Steven's eyes gleamed with excitement.

"That was incredible!"

When Johan first gave him suggestions, he took them seriously, but due to his habits, he hadn't fully implemented or practiced them yet.

But today's battle had given him a real lesson.

It was as if Johan was saying, "Look, Steven—this is how Skarmory should fight."

He realized something important.

The opponent he'd been searching for was right beside him.

Why had Steven been so eager to take over the Devon Corporation?

Was it solely because he worried about his father's health?

Did he not like Pokémon?

No.

He absolutely loved Pokémon, including Pokémon battles.

But when someone doesn't have to put in much effort to be one of the best among their peers, it's easy to lose enthusiasm for it over time.

He loved Pokémon, but he didn't necessarily have to focus on battling, right?

In contrast, taking over a company he wasn't as familiar with presented more of a challenge.

However, today...

He met Lance.

More importantly, he witnessed Johan's strategic brilliance.

He realized that there were skilled people his age after all.

That Johan was so strong!

Not only did Johan excel in theoretical studies, publishing papers that even received Professor Oak's praise, but he was also a Pokémon psychology expert. He raised an exceptional Gastly and demonstrated incredible tactical prowess.

If Johan can excel in so many areas, why can't I?

Johan's "three-pronged strategy" had indeed succeeded—just not in the way he might have expected. Steven's new target had slightly shifted off-course.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gastly's eyes sparkled like stars.

So this is the "Protect-Toxic-Substitute" combo?

It had already mastered Toxic and Substitute; all it needed now was Protect.

Gastly felt that as soon as it mastered that move, under Johan's guidance, it would be—

Super powerful!

It felt incredibly confident now!

Kicking out Champions and taking down Elite Four members one day isn't impossible at all!

Ka-kah-kah~~

"Well done, Skarmory," Johan smiled and extended his hand to Skarmory.

"Kaa~~"

Skarmory shyly turned its head, its cheeks slightly flushed, and lightly tapped Johan's hand with its wing before flying back to Steven.

You were the one who commanded so well.

"Ow—"

Johan grimaced, clutching his now-red hand, feeling the sting.

He then noticed Skarmory's embarrassed demeanor.

So it's a female?

"Johan, your command was exceptional. You've shown me your true strength," Lance approached with softened eyes and a smile, extending his hand. "I look forward to facing you in a real battle someday."

"Don't hold your breath," Johan replied, shaking Lance's hand lightly, given how sore it was.

"But I have a feeling I won't have to wait too long," Lance said, glancing at Gastly, who was clearly brimming with excitement.

"It longs for that moment when you two will astonish the world."

Johan pressed his lips together.

As long as I can afford to "astonish" the world…

"By the way, about Aerodactyl…"

Lance finally remembered the issue with Aerodactyl after his mind had been consumed with thoughts about Protect and countering it.

"There shouldn't be much of an issue. After Skarmory's thorough… um, stimulation, its new goal is clear. The fact that it didn't lose control at the end is proof enough," Johan reassured him.

Lance nodded.

That made sense.

"However, to be safe, I'll record a 'Relaxation Hypnosis' audio clip for you. Just play it for Aerodactyl each day to reinforce its recovery."

"Thank you."

"No problem."

Since he had taken on the task, he was determined to do it well. Fortunately, Aerodactyl's condition wasn't too severe, so a combination of physical treatment and psychological support would suffice.

"Oh, one more thing…"

Lance seemed to remember something, rummaging through his belongings until he produced a pendant shaped like a dragon's claw.

"I didn't really prepare anything for you, so please accept this 'Sharp Fang' as a token of my gratitude."

[Sharp Fang: A held item that grants the Pokémon a chance to make its opponent flinch when using any move that normally wouldn't cause a flinch.]

This item is perfect for Gastly!

"Well, I shouldn't—after all, you already gave me the 'Terror Handbook'..."

Johan adeptly took the Sharp Fang.

"That was the 'treatment fee,' and this is a 'courtesy gift.' It's different."

Seeing Johan accept it, Lance didn't say much more.

Steven and Gastly also joined them.

"Speaking of which, Teacher, I should thank you for the lesson too. Here, I accidentally bought an extra 'Protect' TM last time…"

"Ka-kah!"

Before Johan could respond, Gastly had already snatched the TM with its tongue.

Protect!

"You guys really don't have to…"

Johan accepted the TM from Gastly.

"We're all friends here. Your thoughts alone are enough."

After chatting and exchanging training and battle insights for a while, the trio parted ways.

Over the Hoenn region's seas, Charizard's wings beat steadily, carrying Lance back to the Kanto region.

"Yes, Drake, it's all taken care of. Johan is very skilled, incredibly skilled!"

After hanging up, another call came in. Seeing the name displayed, Lance smiled.

"Winona."

"Yes, everything's fine. He's a Pokémon psychologist, but also a skilled trainer—tactically brilliant. Too bad you couldn't see it."

"Oh? I forgot about that. Even though this wasn't an official mission, the 'field recorder' was still on… Hmm, it's not a mission or classified information, so it's fine to show you…"

He detached a small camera clipped to his shoulder and cape.

"I've sent it."

"Agatha's calling; I'll talk to you later."

In the rural Devon estate…

"Skarmory, let's refine our strategy."

Steven rolled up his sleeves, his eyes alight with enthusiasm.

Not far away, Joseph, accompanied by a floating Castform, smiled as he watched.

"How long has it been since I saw Steven so happy?"

Johan only visited once, and Steven had already rekindled his passion for Pokémon. This reassured Joseph that his decision a few days ago was the right one.

"Woo~~"

Castform happily circled around him.

"Right? Maybe it's time to give Johan a raise?"

"Woo-woo~~"

"Oh, and help Steven get registered for the competition."

"Ka-kah~~"

Gastly followed Johan into the apartment complex, exuding confidence.

"Keep it low-key," Johan couldn't help but say.

"Whether human or ghost, you've got to learn humility. Don't get swayed by superficial things."

Beep, beep—

Johan's phone chimed. When he looked at it, he saw a message.

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: Time for relaxation.

[Grain in the Field]: On my way!

"Ka-kah?" Gastly blinked, puzzled.

Superficial things?

# 50: Can We Get a Subscription?

"Ka-kah?"

Is she asleep?

Gastly, clinging to the ceiling with the "Protect" TM, saw Johan come in from the balcony and asked.

"Yeah," Johan nodded.

He had an odd feeling.

For some reason, it seemed like this "Eevee Eats Cabbage" was even more enthusiastic about hypnotherapy than Feebas was.

But it didn't matter.

In the end, it was all to help Feebas.

And besides, the payment had been quite generous.

"How's 'Protect' coming along? Easy to learn?" Johan asked with a smile as he watched the serious-looking Gastly after finishing up his nightly routine.

Gastly, self-proclaimed as "exceptionally gifted," seemed quite confident. However, mastering "Protect" wasn't the same as learning "Substitute."

"Ka-Kah!"

Of course, it's a piece of cake!

Gastly stuck out its tongue, its eyes flashing with a hint of nervousness, but it still gave a firm response.

Johan didn't want to crush Gastly's confidence, so he simply smiled and turned to his own tasks.

Relieved, Gastly let out a breath.

Close call.

But then it steeled its gaze, determined.

It had to master "Protect," or else its "genius" reputation would crumble!

Floating down to the desk, Gastly carefully opened a metal tin, trying to be as quiet as possible so Johan wouldn't notice.

Inside the box were two types of Pokéblocks left over from the special training in Lavaridge Town—orange and red.

Which one was for boosting special attack?

Whatever.

Both together would be even better!

Gastly gulped, and its tongue darted out.

It swallowed all the remaining Pokéblocks at once.

"Hiss—"

The intense heat and bitterness exploded in its mouth, nearly making it breathe fire.

Gastly:

I regret everything…

But the jolt from the Pokéblocks' spicy, bitter flavors definitely made it more alert!

Meanwhile, Johan was sitting at his desk, flipping through Agatha's Terror Handbook.

The more he read, the more cold sweat began to drip down his forehead.

"Toxic Orb, Black Sludge, Spell Tag, Cursed Amulet, Trevenant's Root, Yamask's Mask…"

All these items combined would cost tens of millions of Pokédollars, easy!

Not to mention the regular expenses and move training costs.

To raise just one Gengar, Agatha must've spent at least 50 million!

Johan glanced at the red-faced, panting Gastly.

Is this really a Pokémon?

This is a gold-eating monster!

"Ka-kah?"

Gastly noticed Johan's stare.

They locked eyes for a moment before both looked away in a panic.

He didn't see me sneak those Pokéblocks, right?

This manual can't fall into Gastly's hands!

Late at night.

In the velvet sky above, a flawless full moon shone with quiet brilliance.

Every so often, a few Zubat flew by the window, hunting the last mosquitoes of autumn. Johan let out a long yawn, eyes tearing up from fatigue.

It had been a long time since he'd stayed up this late reading.

He had to admit, Agatha's Terror Handbook was immensely insightful. It gave him clear guidance on how to train Gastly and a solid plan for its eventual evolution.

"Gastly, aren't you tired yet?"

Closing the Terror Handbook, Johan turned to look at Gastly.

The sight made him jump.

Gastly's eyes were bloodshot, its lips dry, its tongue lolling, and even its dark aura seemed to be fading. It looked utterly exhausted, as if it had been drained eight or nine times over.

"Ka-Kah…Ka-Kah"

Nothing to it…piece of cake…

"Did you…actually master it?" Johan asked, astonished.

"Ka-kah!"

Of course…

Absolutely not!

It was so hard!

Tears welled up in its eyes.

Gastly: (;Д｀)

Johan sighed in relief.

He was really worried Gastly would master "Protect" so quickly, and then he'd have to buy another TM.

"Don't worry, we've got plenty of time. There's no rush," Johan said reassuringly.

"Ka-Kah~~"

I can only get halfway.

"Halfway?" Johan raised an eyebrow. "What do you mean?"

"Ka-Kah!"

Gastly's expression changed, and it concentrated. Black mist swirled around it, and a faint green shield appeared, but it only covered half of Gastly's body.

Still, getting halfway meant the full move couldn't be far off!

"Ka-Kah?"

Seeing Johan's stunned expression, Gastly waved its tongue in front of him.

Slurp—

When Johan didn't respond, Gastly licked his face.

The shock snapped Johan out of it.

Seeing Gastly's puzzled expression, he pulled out a Cheri Berry and took a bite.

"It's nothing. Just suddenly remembered something important."

Feigning nonchalance, Johan took out his phone.

As luck would have it, there were actually messages waiting for him.

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: Someone barged into my house late at night, talking about some new strategy and woke me up. Now I'm wide awake and can't sleep. What should I do?

Someone challenged her at this hour?

Johan winced.

That person was either an idiot or had serious issues.

Seeing the message was from just a few minutes ago, Johan started typing a response. But before he could hit send, another message came in.

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: The moon is so full tonight.

Attached was a photo.

At first glance, it was a beautiful full moon, its pale light illuminating a patch of grass and a lone tree.

The scene was quite serene.

But on closer inspection, cold sweat trickled down Johan's back.

There was a figure hanging from the branches, seemingly in black jeans, with what looked like a red afro, and beneath the tree lay a fainted Houndoom.

What the—

He hesitated, then typed a response—this client paid well and would be essential to raising Gastly.

[Grain in the Field]: Uh, so… do you need a relaxation session?

Johan sighed internally.

How did a Pokémon psychologist end up becoming a sleep therapist…

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: Can I get a subscription? Monthly? Or maybe yearly.

Johan: "???"

Johan cleared his throat awkwardly. "Gastly, don't wander off. I need to make a call."

With that, he headed to the balcony.

"Ka-Kah~~"

Gastly watched him leave, eyes misty with emotion.

Working so late just to raise me… I need to try even harder!

Bathed in moonlight, Johan dialed a voice call.

"Hello~"

The voice on the other end sounded lazy.

But before Johan could say anything, something below caught his eye.

He saw a shadowy figure hurrying across the courtyard, chased by a Golbat and two Zubat. The bats swooped down, occasionally attacking the dark shape.

Golbat chasing a Muk?

What is this, a Pokémon soap opera?

"Hello?" The voice from the phone asked again.

"Hold on a second," Johan muttered, then called into the apartment, "Gastly!"

"Ka-Kah?"

Gastly floated out immediately, curiosity on its face.

Johan gestured downwards and said, "Help it out."

Muk might be a bit of a mess, but it wasn't known for harming people.

Plus, Johan had fed it Pokéblocks before.

Just then, a painful howl came from the shadowy figure.

Wait, that wasn't Muk's voice!

Gastly didn't need any further prompting—it darted down from the balcony in a flash.

Johan stuffed his phone into his pocket and ran out of the apartment.

Following the sounds, he finally found Gastly in a narrow alley, standing protectively in front of a filthy, mud-splattered creature.

Gastly was staring down the Golbat and two Zubat.

Johan approached the creature covered in garbage, noticing a trail of red that stood out starkly against the ground.

"Oowuu…" A weak whimper reached his ears.

Getting closer, despite the awful smell, Johan moved briskly.

He saw six tails matted together with sticky sludge!
